Warning

Running Patroni on memory-restricted systems with Python 3.11+

Unexpected behavior can occur with Python 3.11 or newer on systems with strict memory limits. For example, this includes vm.overcommit_memory=2, which is recommended for PostgreSQL. You may observe these symptoms:

  • Patroni appears healthy
  • PostgreSQL continues to run
  • Patroni REST API becomes unresponsive
  • The operating system reports that Patroni is listening on the REST API port
  • Patroni logs look normal; however, following messages may appear once: Exception ignored in thread started by: <object repr() failed>, MemoryError
  • Kernel logs may contain messages such as not enough memory for the allocation

A bug in Python 3.11+ causes this behavior. Under strict memory conditions, a new thread may hang indefinitely if free memory is insufficient.

Recommended solution

Recent Patroni releases (4.1.1+, 4.0.8+) start all required threads early, before the system has memory pressure. This reduces the impact of the issue.

Additional recommendations (Linux, glibc)

When you use vm.overcommit_memory=2 (recommended for PostgreSQL), we also recommend these environment variables before you start Patroni:

  • MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=1 - reduces the amount of virtual memory allocated by glibc for multi-threaded applications
  • PG_MALLOC_ARENA_MAX= - resets the value of MALLOC_ARENA_MAX for PostgreSQL processes started by Patroni.

In addition, you may tune the following Patroni configuration parameters:

  • thread_stack_size - Stack size for threads that Patroni starts. A lower value reduces Patroni memory usage. The default is 512kB. Increase thread_stack_size if Patroni has stack-related crashes. Otherwise, the default is sufficient.
  • thread_pool_size - Thread pool size for asynchronous tasks and REST API communication with other members during leader races or failsafe checks. The default is 5, which is sufficient for three-node clusters.
  • restapi.thread_pool_size - Thread pool size for REST API requests. The default is 5, which allows up to five parallel requests. Requests with SQL queries effectively run in sequence because they use one database connection. A higher value typically gives no benefit.

Patroni is a Python template for high availability (HA) PostgreSQL solutions. It supports distributed configuration stores such as ZooKeeper, etcd, Consul, and Kubernetes. Database engineers, DBAs, DevOps engineers, and SREs can use it to deploy HA PostgreSQL quickly in datacenters or other environments.

Patroni is a template. Its replication system needs configuration for your requirements and has limitations that you must consider. PostgreSQL supports many high-availability approaches. See the PostgreSQL Documentation for a list.

Currently supported PostgreSQL versions: 9.3 to 18.

Note to Citus users: Patroni 3.0 and later integrate with the Citus database extension to Postgres. See the Citus support page for Patroni high availability with a Citus distributed cluster.

Note to Kubernetes users: Patroni can run natively on Kubernetes. See the Kubernetes chapter.

1 - Introduction

Patroni introduction, quick start, and core high-availability concepts.

Source: https://patroni.readthedocs.io/en/latest/README.html

Patroni is a Python template for high availability (HA) PostgreSQL solutions. It started as a fork of Governor, a Compose project, and adds many features.

For additional background info, see:

  • PostgreSQL HA with Kubernetes and Patroni, talk by Josh Berkus at KubeCon 2016 (video)
  • Feb. 2016 Zalando Tech blog post

Development Status

Patroni is in active development and accepts contributions. See Contributing for details.

See release information for new releases.

Technical Requirements/Installation

See the installation guide to install and upgrade Patroni on different platforms.

Planning the Number of PostgreSQL Nodes

Patroni/PostgreSQL nodes are independent of DCS nodes, except when Patroni implements RAFT itself. Thus, there is no minimum node count for Patroni/PostgreSQL. A cluster can have one primary and one standby. You can add more standby nodes later.

2-node clusters (primary + standby) are common and provide automatic failover with high availability. During failover, the cluster temporarily has no redundancy until the failed node rejoins.

DCS requirements: Your DCS (etcd, ZooKeeper, Consul) must have 3 or 5 nodes for consensus and fault tolerance. One DCS cluster can store data for hundreds or thousands of Patroni clusters. Use different namespace/scope combinations for these clusters.

Running and Configuring

This section assumes you cloned the Patroni repository from https://github.com/patroni/patroni. You need the example configuration files postgres0.yml and postgres1.yml. If you installed Patroni with pip, get these files from the git repository. Then replace ./patroni.py below with the patroni command.

To start, run these commands in separate terminals:

> etcd --data-dir=data/etcd --enable-v2=true
> ./patroni.py postgres0.yml
> ./patroni.py postgres1.yml

These commands start a high-availability cluster. Test different YAML settings to see how they change cluster behavior. Stop some components to test how the system responds.

Add more postgres*.yml files to create an even larger cluster.

Patroni provides an HAProxy configuration. It gives your application one endpoint to connect to the cluster leader. To configure it, run:

> haproxy -f haproxy.cfg

> psql --host 127.0.0.1 --port 5000 postgres

YAML Configuration

See YAML settings for details about etcd, consul, and ZooKeeper settings. See postgres0.yml for an example.

Environment Configuration

See environment settings to configure or override settings with environment variables.

Replication Choices

Patroni uses Postgres streaming replication, which is asynchronous by default. Set maximum_lag_on_failover to prevent failover when a replica is more than the specified number of bytes behind the leader. Adjust this setting for your business requirements. You can also use synchronous replication for stronger durability guarantees. See replication modes documentation for details.

Applications Should Not Use Superusers

Always use a non-superuser for application connections. Patroni needs database access to operate correctly. An application that uses a superuser can consume all connections, including those reserved by superuser_reserved_connections. If no connections remain, Patroni cannot access the primary and can behave incorrectly.

Testing Your HA Solution

HA testing takes time and involves many variables, especially for a cross-platform application. A trained system administrator or consultant must do this work. These documents do not cover the process in detail.

Test these parts of your infrastructure:

  • Network (the network in front of your system as well as the NICs physicalorvirtualphysical or virtualphysicalorvirtualthemselves)
  • Disk IO
  • file limits (nofile in Linux)
  • RAM. Even if you have oomkiller turned off, the unavailability of RAM could cause issues.
  • CPU
  • Virtualization Contention (overcommitting the hypervisor)
  • Any cgroup limitation (likely to be related to the above)
  • kill -9 of any postgres process (except postmaster!). This is a decent simulation of a segfault.

Do not run kill -9 on a postmaster process. This does not simulate a real-world failure. If an attacker can run kill -9, an HA process cannot resolve the security problem. The attacker can kill the process again or cause other damage.

2 - Installation

Installation and upgrade instructions for Patroni across supported platforms.

Source: https://patroni.readthedocs.io/en/latest/installation.html

Pre-requirements for Mac OS

On a Mac, install the requirements:

brew install postgresql etcd haproxy libyaml python

Psycopg

Since psycopg2-2.8, the binary package is not installed by default. A source installation requires a C compiler and Postgres and Python development packages. Python dependencies cannot express psycopg2 OR psycopg2-binary. Select an installation method:

  1. Use the package manager from your distro
sudo apt-get install python3-psycopg2  # install psycopg2 module on Debian/Ubuntu
sudo yum install python3-psycopg2      # install psycopg2 on RedHat/Fedora/CentOS
  1. Specify one of psycopg, psycopg2, or psycopg2-binary in the list of dependencies when installing Patroni with pip.

General installation for pip

Patroni can be installed with pip:

pip install patroni[dependencies]

Leave dependencies empty, or specify one or more of these dependencies:

etcd or etcd3
python-etcd module to use Etcd as the Distributed Configuration Store (DCS)

consul
py-consul module to use Consul as the DCS

zookeeper
kazoo module to use Zookeeper as the DCS

exhibitor
kazoo module to use Exhibitor as the DCS (same dependencies as Zookeeper)

kubernetes
kubernetes module to use Kubernetes as the DCS

raft
pysyncobj module to use the Python Raft implementation as the DCS

aws
boto3 to use AWS callbacks

jsonlogger
python-json-logger module for JSON logging

systemd
systemd-python for sd_notify integration

all
all of the above (except psycopg family)

psycopg3
psycopg\[binary\]\>=3.0.0 module

psycopg2
psycopg2\>=2.5.4 module

psycopg2-binary
psycopg2-binary module

For example, install Patroni with psycopg3, Etcd DCS dependencies, and AWS callbacks:

pip install patroni[psycopg3,etcd3,aws]

Install external tools, such as WAL-E, separately if your replica creation or custom bootstrap scripts call them.

Package installation on Linux

The Postgres community may provide Patroni packages for these operating systems:

  • RHEL, RockyLinux, AlmaLinux;
  • Debian and Ubuntu;
  • SUSE Enterprise Linux.

Packages may also provide direct dependencies, such as Python modules absent from the operating system repositories.

For more information see the PGDG repository documentation.

On RedHat Enterprise Linux derivatives, you may also need EPEL packages. See the EPEL repository documentation.

After you configure the PGDG repository for your operating system, install Patroni.

Note

The Postgres community maintains these packages, rather than the Patroni developers. For support, first contact Postgres slack.

Installing on Debian derivatives

After you configure the PGDG repository, install Patroni with apt:

apt-get install patroni

Installing on RedHat derivatives

On RHEL 9 and derivatives, first configure the PGDG repository. Then install Patroni with an etcd DCS:

dnf install patroni patroni-etcd

If your RedHat derivative does not provide etcd packages, install them from PGDG. On each DCS node, run:

dnf install 'dnf-command(config-manager)'
dnf config-manager --enable pgdg-rhel9-extras
dnf install etcd

For RHEL 8, replace the version with 8 to use pgdg-rhel8-extras. RockyLinux, AlmaLinux, Oracle Linux, and other derivatives also use the pgdg-rhelN-extras repository name.

Installing on SUSE Enterprise Linux

Some dependencies may require SUSE PackageHub repositories. See the SUSE PackageHub documentation.

On SLES 15, configure the PGDG repository. Then install Patroni:

zypper install patroni patroni-etcd

With the SUSE PackageHub repo enabled you can also install etcd:

SUSEConnect -p PackageHub/15.5/x86_64
zypper install etcd

Upgrading

To upgrade Patroni, update the software and restart the Patroni daemon on each cluster node.

A Patroni daemon restart also restarts the Postgres database. This can cause primary failover. We recommend maintenance mode until all Patroni daemon restarts finish.

On one Patroni node, put the cluster in maintenance mode:

patronictl pause --wait

Then on each node in the cluster, perform the package upgrade required for your OS:

apt-get update && apt-get install patroni patroni-etcd

Restart the patroni daemon process on each node:

systemctl restart patroni

Resume Patroni monitoring and leave maintenance mode:

patronictl resume --wait

The cluster now operates fully with the new Patroni version.

3 - Patroni configuration

Patroni configuration model, precedence rules, and validation tooling.

Source: https://patroni.readthedocs.io/en/latest/patroni_configuration.html

There are 3 types of Patroni configuration:

  • Global dynamic configuration.
    These options are stored in the DCS (Distributed Configuration Store) and apply to all nodes. Change them at any time with patronictl_edit_config or the REST API. Changes outside startup configuration apply asynchronously on each node’s next wake-up cycle, followed by a reload. Changed PostgreSQL parameters with postmaster context require a restart. Patroni sets pending_restart in members.data JSON for these nodes. Node status also shows "restart_pending": true.

  • Local configuration file (patroni.yml).
    Local options override dynamic configuration. To reload patroni.yml without restarting Patroni, send SIGHUP, send POST /reload, or run patronictl_reload. Local configuration can be one YAML file or a directory. Patroni loads all YAML files in a directory in sorted order. If several files define a key, the last file takes precedence.

  • Environment configuration.
    Environment variables can set or override some local parameters. Use them in dynamic environments where some values are unknown in advance. For example, the external IP address may be unknown inside docker.

Important rules

PostgreSQL parameters controlled by Patroni

These PostgreSQL parameters must have the same values on the primary and replicas. Local configuration and environment values have no effect. Set them through shared DCS configuration. The list shows their defaults and minimum values:

  • max_connections: default value 100, minimal value 25
  • max_locks_per_transaction: default value 64, minimal value 32
  • max_worker_processes: default value 8, minimal value 2
  • max_prepared_transactions: default value 0, minimal value 0
  • wal_level: default value hot_standby, accepted values: hot_standby, replica, logical
  • track_commit_timestamp: default value off

PostgreSQL permits different primary and replica values for the parameters below. However, a replica can become primary at any time. Different values are therefore unsuitable, and Patroni permits changes only through dynamic configuration.

  • max_wal_senders: default value 10, minimal value 3
  • max_replication_slots: default value 10, minimal value 4
  • wal_keep_segments: default value 8, minimal value 1
  • wal_keep_size: default value 128MB, minimal value 16MB
  • wal_log_hints: on

Patroni validates these values for correctness or a required minimum.

There are some other Postgres parameters controlled by Patroni:

  • listen_addresses - is set either from postgresql.listen or from PATRONI_POSTGRESQL_LISTEN environment variable
  • port - is set either from postgresql.listen or from PATRONI_POSTGRESQL_LISTEN environment variable
  • cluster_name - is set either from scope or from PATRONI_SCOPE environment variable
  • hot_standby: on

Patroni writes the parameters above to postgresql.conf. Except for wal_keep_segments and wal_keep_size, it also passes them as postgres arguments. These arguments take precedence over all other settings, including ALTER SYSTEM.

Parameters such as postgresql.listen and postgresql.data_dir can only be set locally. Use the configuration file or environment variables. Local configuration overrides dynamic configuration in most cases.

When applying the local or dynamic configuration options, the following actions are taken:

  • The node checks for postgresql.base.conf and custom_conf.
  • If custom_conf is set, its file supplies the base configuration. Patroni ignores postgresql.base.conf and postgresql.conf as base files.
  • Otherwise, an existing postgresql.base.conf supplies the base configuration. It contains the renamed original configuration.
  • If neither exists, Patroni renames the original postgresql.conf to postgresql.base.conf and uses it as the base.
  • Patroni writes dynamic options to postgresql.conf, subject to the exceptions above. It adds an include for postgresql.base.conf or the custom_conf file. New options can then apply without rereading the file to check for the include.
  • Command-line arguments override parameters essential for Patroni cluster management.
  • If a changed value requires restart according to pg_settings context, Patroni sets pending_restart on the node. Any restart resets this flag.

The parameters would be applied in the following order (run-time are given the highest priority):

  1. load parameters from file postgresql.base.conf (or from a custom_conf file, if set)
  2. load parameters from file postgresql.conf
  3. load parameters from file postgresql.auto.conf
  4. run-time parameter using -o --name=value

This order supports shared node configuration (2) and node-specific ALTER SYSTEM settings (3). It enforces essential Patroni parameters (4). It also permits external tools to manage the base configuration directly (1).

PostgreSQL parameters that touch shared memory

PostgreSQL has some parameters that determine the size of the shared memory used by them:

  • max_connections
  • max_prepared_transactions
  • max_locks_per_transaction
  • max_wal_senders
  • max_worker_processes

Changes require a PostgreSQL restart. Standby shared memory structures must not be smaller than the corresponding primary structures.

Change these values through dynamic configuration, as Patroni requires. The usual sequence is:

  1. Applying changes through patronictl_edit_config (or via REST API /config endpoint)
  2. Restarting nodes through patronictl_restart (or via REST API /restart endpoint)

Note: Restart PostgreSQL through patronictl_restart or the REST API /restart endpoint. Restarting the Patroni daemon with systemctl restart patroni can trigger failover if the node is primary.

These shared memory settings require a specific restart order:

  • If you want to increase the value of any of those settings:

    1. Restart all standbys first
    2. Restart the primary after that

  • If you want to decrease the value of any of those settings:

    1. Restart the primary first
    2. Restart all standbys after that

Note: If you restart all nodes together after a decrease, Patroni restarts standbys with the original value. You must restart them again later. This prevents an endless crash loop: PostgreSQL exits with FATAL if a value is below that recorded in standby pg_controldata. A standby value can decrease only after its pg_controldata reflects the primary change.

More information about that can be found at PostgreSQL Administrator’s Overview.

Patroni configuration parameters

Also the following Patroni configuration options can be changed only dynamically:

  • ttl: 30
  • loop_wait: 10
  • retry_timeout: 10
  • maximum_lag_on_failover: 1048576
  • max_timelines_history: 0
  • check_timeline: false
  • postgresql.use_slots: true

After a change, Patroni reads the relevant DCS configuration section and updates its runtime values.

After each configuration change, Patroni writes DCS options to patroni.dynamic.json in the Postgres data directory. Only the leader can restore these options from disk. It can do so only when DCS options are absent or invalid.

Configuration generation and validation

The patroni executable can generate and validate local configuration. Use it to:

  • Create a sample local Patroni configuration;
  • Create a Patroni configuration file for the locally running PostgreSQL instance (e.g. as a preparation step for the Patroni integration);
  • Validate a given Patroni configuration file.

Sample Patroni configuration

patroni --generate-sample-config [configfile]

Description

Generate a sample Patroni configuration in yaml format. Values come from Environment configuration. For unset parameters, use Patroni defaults or #FIXME for values that the user must supply.

Some default values are defined based on the local setup:

  • postgresql.listen: the IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the standard 5432 port.
  • postgresql.connect_address: the IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the standard 5432 port.
  • postgresql.authentication.rewind: is only defined if the PostgreSQL version can be defined from the binary and the version is 11 or later.
  • restapi.listen: IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the standard 8008 port.
  • restapi.connect_address: IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the standard 8008 port.

Parameters

configfile - full path to the configuration file used to store the result. If not provided, the result is sent to stdout.

Patroni configuration for a running instance

patroni --generate-config [--dsn DSN] [configfile]

Description

Generate a yaml configuration for the local running PostgreSQL instance. Connection settings use the supplied DSN first, then PostgreSQL environment variables. If no password is supplied, enter it at the prompt.

All non-internal GUCs from the source instance supply these Patroni parameters. This includes GUCs set through files, postmaster command-line arguments, or environment variables:

  • scope: cluster_name GUC value;
  • postgresql.listen: listen_addresses and port GUC values;
  • postgresql.datadir: data_directory GUC value;
  • postgresql.parameters: archive_command, restore_command, archive_cleanup_command, recovery_end_command, ssl_passphrase_command, hba_file, ident_file, config_file GUC values;
  • bootstrap.dcs: all other gathered PostgreSQL GUCs.

If Postgres GUCs do not supply scope, postgresql.listen, or postgresql.datadir, Patroni uses the corresponding Environment configuration value.

Other rules applied for the values definition:

  • name: PATRONI_NAME environment variable value if set, otherwise the current machine’s hostname.
  • postgresql.bin_dir: path to the Postgres binaries gathered from the running instance.
  • postgresql.connect_address: the IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the port used for the instance connection or the port GUC value.
  • postgresql.authentication.superuser: the configuration used for the instance connection;
  • postgresql.pg_hba: the lines gathered from the source instance’s hba_file.
  • postgresql.pg_ident: the lines gathered from the source instance’s ident_file.
  • restapi.listen: IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the standard 8008 port.
  • restapi.connect_address: IP address returned by gethostname call for the current machine’s hostname and the standard 8008 port.

The generated file also includes other parameters from Environment configuration.

Parameters

configfile
Full path to the configuration file used to store the result. If not provided, result is sent to stdout.

dsn
Optional DSN string for the local PostgreSQL instance to get GUC values from.

Validate Patroni configuration

patroni --validate-config [configfile] [--ignore-listen-port | -i]

Description

Validate the Patroni configuration and print failed checks.

Parameters

configfile
Full path to the file to check. If absent or nonexistent, read PATRONI_CONFIG_VARIABLE. If that variable is unset, use Patroni environment variables.

--ignore-listen-port | -i
Optional flag to ignore bind failures for occupied listen ports during configfile validation.

--print | -p
Optional flag to print local configuration, including environment overrides, after successful validation.

3.1 - Dynamic Configuration Settings

Dynamic configuration settings stored in DCS and applied cluster-wide.

Source: https://patroni.readthedocs.io/en/latest/dynamic_configuration.html

Dynamic configuration is stored in the DCS (Distributed Configuration Store) and applied on all cluster nodes.

Change dynamic configuration with patronictl_edit_config or the Patroni REST API.

  • loop_wait: the number of seconds the loop will sleep. Default value: 10, minimum possible value: 1
  • ttl: leader lock TTL, in seconds. This is the interval before automatic failover can start. Default: 30. Minimum: 20.
  • retry_timeout: timeout for DCS and PostgreSQL operation retries (in seconds). DCS or network issues shorter than this will not cause Patroni to demote the leader. Default value: 10, minimum possible value: 3
Warning

when changing values of loop_wait, retry_timeout, or ttl you have to follow the rule:

loop_wait + 2 * retry_timeout <= ttl
  • maximum_lag_on_failover: the maximum bytes a follower may lag to be able to participate in leader election.
  • primary_race_backoff: delays standby leader races by primary_race_backoff seconds while WAL replication from the primary advances. This reduces unnecessary failovers when Patroni is briefly unresponsive. Default: 0 (disabled).
  • maximum_lag_on_syncnode: maximum synchronous replica lag in bytes before replacement by a healthy asynchronous replica. With multiple replicas, Patroni uses the maximum replica LSN. Otherwise, it uses the leader’s current WAL LSN. Default: -1. Values of 0 or less disable replacement. Use a sufficiently high value to avoid frequent replacements during high transaction volume.
  • max_timelines_history: maximum number of timeline history items kept in DCS. Default value: 0. When set to 0, it keeps the full history in DCS.
  • primary_start_timeout: seconds allowed for primary recovery before failover. Default: 300. A value of 0 permits immediate failover after crash detection. Asynchronous failover can lose transactions. Worst-case failover time is loop_wait + primary_start_timeout + loop_wait, or only loop_wait when primary_start_timeout is zero. Select the value for your durability and availability requirements.
  • primary_stop_timeout: seconds Patroni can wait for Postgres shutdown when synchronous_mode is enabled. With synchronous_mode enabled and a value > 0, Patroni sends SIGKILL to postmaster if shutdown exceeds this interval. Select the value for your durability and availability requirements. Unset values or values <= 0 disable this timeout.
  • synchronous_mode: synchronous replication mode: off, on, or quorum. The leader manages synchronous_standby_names. Only the last known leader or synchronous replicas can enter a leader race. This protects successful commits during failover but can block writes when Patroni cannot guarantee durability. See replication modes documentation.
  • synchronous_mode_strict: keeps synchronous replication enabled when no synchronous replica is available, blocking client writes. Without an eligible streaming replica, Patroni uses the last synchronous nodes from /sync for synchronous_standby_names. If no prior state exists, it uses __patroni_strict_sync_replica_placeholder__. Do not use that reserved value for the node name in patroni.yaml. See replication modes documentation.
  • synchronous_node_count: required synchronous standby count when synchronous_mode is enabled. Patroni updates the DCS and PostgreSQL synchronous_standby_names as members join or leave. It reduces the count if it exceeds the number of eligible nodes. Default: 1.
  • failsafe_mode: Enables DCS Failsafe Mode. Defaults to false.
  • postgresql:
    • use_pg_rewind: enables pg_rewind. Default: false. For correct operation, pg_rewind requires data page checksums and/or wal_log_hints set to on. Enable checksums with the --data-checksums option for initdb.
    • use_slots: whether or not to use replication slots. Defaults to true on PostgreSQL 9.4+.
    • recovery_conf: extra recovery.conf settings for replicas. PostgreSQL 12 no longer uses recovery.conf. You can still use this section because Patroni handles the version difference.
    • parameters: configuration parameters (GUCs) for Postgres in format {max_connections: 100, wal_level: "replica", max_wal_senders: 10, wal_log_hints: "on"}. Many of these are required for replication to work.
    • parameters_primary: (optional) role-specific parameter overrides for primary. These values are merged with and override the base parameters.
    • parameters_replica: (optional) role-specific parameter overrides for replica. These values are merged with and override the base parameters.
    • parameters_standby_leader: (optional) role-specific parameter overrides for standby_leader. These values are merged with and override the base parameters.
    • pg_hba: list of lines that Patroni will use to generate pg_hba.conf. Patroni ignores this parameter if hba_file PostgreSQL parameter is set to a non-default value.
      • - host all all 0.0.0.0/0 md5
      • - host replication replicator 127.0.0.1/32 md5: A line like this is required for replication.
    • pg_hba_primary: (optional) role-specific pg_hba entries for primary. These completely replace pg_hba (no merging). If not defined, pg_hba is used.
    • pg_hba_replica: (optional) role-specific pg_hba entries for replica. These completely replace pg_hba (no merging). If not defined, pg_hba is used.
    • pg_hba_standby_leader: (optional) role-specific pg_hba entries for standby_leader. These completely replace pg_hba (no merging). If not defined, pg_hba is used.
    • pg_ident: list of lines that Patroni will use to generate pg_ident.conf. Patroni ignores this parameter if ident_file PostgreSQL parameter is set to a non-default value.
      • - mapname1 systemname1 pguser1
      • - mapname1 systemname2 pguser2
    • pg_ident_primary: (optional) role-specific pg_ident entries for primary. These completely replace pg_ident (no merging). If not defined, pg_ident is used.
    • pg_ident_replica: (optional) role-specific pg_ident entries for replica. These completely replace pg_ident (no merging). If not defined, pg_ident is used.
    • pg_ident_standby_leader: (optional) role-specific pg_ident entries for standby_leader. These completely replace pg_ident (no merging). If not defined, pg_ident is used.
  • standby_cluster: if this section is defined, we want to bootstrap a standby cluster.
    • host: an address of remote node
    • port: a port of remote node
    • primary_slot_name: replication slot on the remote node. Optional. The default comes from the instance name; see slot_name_from_member_name.
    • create_replica_methods: ordered methods to bootstrap the standby leader from the remote primary. This list can differ from postgresql_settings.
    • restore_command: command to restore remote primary WAL records to standby cluster nodes. It can differ from postgresql_settings.
    • archive_cleanup_command: cleanup command for standby leader
    • recovery_min_apply_delay: how long to wait before actually apply WAL records on a standby leader
  • member_slots_ttl: physical slot retention after replicas shut down. Default: 30min. A value of 0 restores immediate removal when the member key expires from the DCS. Requires PostgreSQL 11 or later.
  • slots: permanent replication slots, retained through switchover and failover. Patroni creates missing slots. On PostgreSQL 11 and later, it creates physical slots on all nodes and advances them every loop_wait seconds. Earlier versions maintain permanent physical slots only on the primary. Logical slots are copied from the primary to a standby with restart. Their positions then advance every loop_wait seconds if necessary. Patroni copies logical slot files through libpq with rewind or superuser credentials; see postgresql.authentication. A replica slot can lag behind the old primary. Applications must handle repeated messages after failover, for example by tracking confirmed_flush_lsn. Permanent slots require postgresql.use_slots set to true. Permanent logical slots automatically enable hot_standby_feedback. Logical slot failover is unsafe on PostgreSQL 9.6 and older, and PostgreSQL 10 lacks required functions. This feature requires PostgreSQL 11+.
    • my_slot_name: permanent slot name. Patroni does not create a slot on the node with the same name. A permanent physical slot named after a member remains even when that member becomes unresponsive. Normally, Patroni would remove it. This preserves member slots during temporary failures or migration to Patroni; see Convert a Standalone to a Patroni Cluster. Remove these matching names from DCS configuration when the slots are no longer required. Otherwise, they affect normal Patroni operation.
      • type: physical or logical. Logical slots also require database and plugin. Physical slots optionally accept cluster_type.
      • database: the database name where logical slots should be created.
      • plugin: the plugin name for the logical slot.
      • cluster_type: create the slot only on this cluster type: primary or standby. On other types, Patroni skips creation or drops an existing slot.
  • ignore_slots: sets of slot properties that Patroni must ignore. Use this for slots managed outside Patroni. Any matching subset of properties causes a slot to be ignored.
    • name: the name of the replication slot.
    • type: slot type. Can be physical or logical. If the slot is logical, you may additionally define database and/or plugin.
    • database: the database name (when matching a logical slot).
    • plugin: the logical decoding plugin (when matching a logical slot).

Note: slots is a hashmap while ignore_slots is an array. For example:

slots:
  permanent_logical_slot_name:
    type: logical
    database: my_db
    plugin: test_decoding
  permanent_physical_slot_name:
    type: physical
  ...
ignore_slots:
  - name: ignored_logical_slot_name
    type: logical
    database: my_db
    plugin: test_decoding
  - name: ignored_physical_slot_name
    type: physical
  ...

Note: On PostgreSQL v11 or later, Patroni maintains physical slots on all nodes eligible to become leader. Replicas therefore retain WAL segments that other nodes may need. After an absent node’s DCS member key expires, Patroni drops its slot after member_slots_ttl, which defaults to 30min. Adjust retention for your requirements.

For a fixed topology with stable node names, you can configure permanent physical slots named after the nodes. This prevents slot removal and WAL recycling while a replica is temporarily down:

slots:
  node_name1:
    type: physical
  node_name2:
    type: physical
  node_name3:
    type: physical
  ...
Warning

Permanent slots synchronize only from primary/standby_leader to replicas. Applications must therefore use them only on the leader. Using them on replicas causes indefinite pg_wal growth on all other nodes. Physical slots named after Patroni members are an exception. Patroni creates and synchronizes these slots across all nodes for member replication.

Warning

The nostream tag disables permanent logical slot copying and synchronization on a standby and all its cascading replicas.

3.2 - YAML Configuration Settings

Complete reference for Patroni YAML configuration options and sections.

Source: https://patroni.readthedocs.io/en/latest/yaml_configuration.html

Global/Universal

  • thread_pool_size: thread pool size for asynchronous tasks and REST API communication with other members during leader races or failsafe checks. Minimum: 5. Default: 5.
  • thread_stack_size: stack size for Patroni threads. Use a multiple of 64kB. Minimum: 64kB. Patroni default: 512kB.
  • name: the name of the host. Must be unique for the cluster. The value __patroni_strict_sync_replica_placeholder__ is reserved for internal use by Patroni and cannot be used as a node name.
  • namespace: path within the configuration store where Patroni will keep information about the cluster. Default value: “/service”
  • scope: cluster name

Log

  • type: sets the format of logs. Can be either plain or json. To use json format, you must have the jsonlogger installed. The default value is plain.
  • level: sets the general logging level. Default value is INFO (see the docs for Python logging)
  • traceback_level: sets the level where tracebacks will be visible. Default value is ERROR. Set it to DEBUG if you want to see tracebacks only if you enable log.level=DEBUG.
  • format: log format. For plain logs, use a string. For json, use a string or a list of LogRecord attributes. In lists, use field names without %( and ). To rename a field, use a dictionary with the original field as key and the output name as value. Default: %(asctime)s %(levelname)s: %(message)s.
  • dateformat: sets the datetime formatting string. (see the formatTime() documentation)
  • static_fields: add additional fields to the log. This option is only available when the log type is set to json.
  • max_queue_size: maximum internal log queue size. Patroni writes records to an in-memory queue. A separate thread reads them and writes to stderr or a file. The default 1000 records retain approximately 1h20m of logs.
  • dir: application log directory. It must exist and be writable by the Patroni user. By default, Patroni retains four 25MB files. Adjust retention with file_num and file_size.
  • mode: Permissions for log files (for example, 0644). If not specified, permissions will be set based on the current umask value.
  • file_num: The number of application logs to retain.
  • file_size: Size of patroni.log file (in bytes) that triggers a log rolling.
  • loggers: This section allows redefining logging level per python module
    • patroni.postmaster: WARNING
    • urllib3: DEBUG
  • deduplicate_heartbeat_logs: suppresses consecutive identical heartbeat messages when true. Default: false.
Warning

HA loop timing helps diagnose failovers caused by exhausted resources and similar problems. With deduplicate_heartbeat_logs set to true, loop execution produces no log unless the leader changes. These diagnostics are then unavailable in the logs.

For JSON logging, use this example:

log:
   type: json
   format:
      - message
      - module
      - asctime: '@timestamp'
      - levelname: level
   static_fields:
      app: patroni

Bootstrap configuration

Note

After initial cluster bootstrap, Patroni stores settings in the DCS. Later changes to YAML bootstrap.dcs have no effect. Use patronictl_edit_config or the REST API to change them.

  • bootstrap:

    • dcs: global dynamic cluster configuration. Put parameters from Dynamic Configuration settings under bootstrap.dcs. After bootstrap, Patroni writes this section to /<namespace>/<scope>/config in the configuration store.

    • method: custom script to use for bootstrapping this cluster.

      See custom bootstrap methods. Set method to initdb, or omit it, to use the default initdb command.

    • initdb: (optional) list options to be passed on to initdb.

      • - data-checksums: Must be enabled when pg_rewind is needed on 9.3.
      • - encoding: UTF8: default encoding for new databases.
      • - locale: UTF8: default locale for new databases.

    • post_bootstrap or post_init: script to run after cluster initialization. It receives a connection URL with the cluster superuser name. PGPASSFILE points to the pgpass file.

Citus

Enable Citus integration. Patroni then registers workers on the coordinator. See Citus support.

  • group: the Citus group id, integer. Use 0 for coordinator and 1, 2, etc… for workers
  • database: database for the citus extension. Use the same database on the coordinator and all workers. Currently, only one database is supported.

Consul

Most of the parameters are optional, but you have to specify one of the host or url

  • host: the host:port for the Consul local agent.
  • url: url for the Consul local agent, in format: http(s)://host:port.
  • port: (optional) Consul port.
  • scheme: (optional) http or https, defaults to http.
  • token: (optional) ACL token.
  • verify: (optional) whether to verify the SSL certificate for HTTPS requests.
  • cacert: (optional) The ca certificate. If present it will enable validation.
  • cert: (optional) file with the client certificate.
  • key: (optional) file with the client key. Can be empty if the key is part of cert.
  • dc: (optional) Datacenter to communicate with. By default the datacenter of the host is used.
  • consistency: (optional) Select consul consistency mode. Possible values are default, consistent, or stale (more details in consul API reference)
  • checks: (optional) list of Consul health checks used for the session. By default an empty list is used.
  • register_service: (optional) registers a service named by scope. Its role tag is master, primary, replica, or standby-leader. Default: false.
  • service_tags: (optional) additional static tags to add to the Consul service apart from the role (primary/replica/standby-leader). By default an empty list is used.
  • service_check_interval: (optional) how often to perform health check against registered url. Defaults to ‘5s’.
  • service_check_tls_server_name: (optional) override SNI host when connecting via TLS, see also consul agent check API reference.

The token needs to have the following ACL permissions:

service_prefix "${scope}" {
    policy = "write"
}
key_prefix "${namespace}/${scope}" {
    policy = "write"
}
session_prefix "" {
    policy = "write"
}

Etcd

Most of the parameters are optional, but you have to specify one of the host, hosts, url, proxy or srv

  • host: the host:port for the etcd endpoint.
  • hosts: list of etcd endpoint in format host1:port1,host2:port2,etc… Could be a comma separated string or an actual yaml list.
  • use_proxies: if true, treats hosts as proxies. Patroni does not discover etcd topology.
  • url: url for the etcd.
  • proxy: proxy url for the etcd. If you are connecting to the etcd using proxy, use this parameter instead of url.
  • srv: domain for SRV-based cluster discovery. Patroni queries these services in order until one succeeds: _etcd-client-ssl, _etcd-client, _etcd-ssl, _etcd, _etcd-server-ssl, _etcd-server. For _etcd-server-ssl or _etcd-server, it queries available members through the ETCD peer protocol. Otherwise, it uses hosts from the SRV records.
  • srv_suffix: suffix for discovery SRV names. Requires srv. Use it to distinguish etcd clusters in one domain. For example, srv_suffix: foo and srv: example.org produce _etcd-client-ssl-foo._tcp.example.com. Patroni applies this suffix to each possible ETCD SRV service name.
  • protocol: (optional) http or https, if not specified http is used. If the url or proxy is specified - will take protocol from them.
  • username: (optional) username for etcd authentication.
  • password: (optional) password for etcd authentication.
  • cacert: (optional) The ca certificate. If present it will enable validation.
  • cert: (optional) file with the client certificate.
  • key: (optional) file with the client key. Can be empty if the key is part of cert.

Etcdv3

To use Etcd protocol version 3, configure the etcd3 section. Its parameters are the same as etcd.

Warning

Protocol versions 2 and 3 cannot see each other’s keys. Changing Patroni configuration alone cannot migrate etcd to etcd3. Patroni uses the Etcd gRPC-gateway proxy for the V3 API. This prevents TLS common name authentication.

ZooKeeper

  • hosts: List of ZooKeeper cluster members in format: ′host1:port1′,′host2:port2′,′etc...′'host1:port1', 'host2:port2', 'etc...'′host1:port1′,′host2:port2′,′etc...′.
  • use_ssl: (optional) Whether SSL is used or not. Defaults to false. If set to false, all SSL specific parameters are ignored.
  • cacert: (optional) The CA certificate. If present it will enable validation.
  • cert: (optional) File with the client certificate.
  • key: (optional) File with the client key.
  • key_password: (optional) The client key password.
  • verify: (optional) Whether to verify certificate or not. Defaults to true.
  • set_acls: (optional) default ACL that Kazoo applies to each new ZNode. Use x509 (default) or another supported ZooKeeper scheme, such as digest. Supply a dictionary: each key is the full principal, optionally prefixed with its scheme. Each value lists one or more permissions: CREATE, READ, WRITE, DELETE, ADMIN, or ALL. Example: set_acls: {CN=principal1: [CREATE, READ], digest:principal2:+pjROuBuuwNNSujKyH8dGcEnFPQ=: [ALL]}.
  • auth_data: (optional) connection authentication dictionary. Use scheme as the key and credential as the value. Default: empty dictionary.
Note

It is required to install kazoo>=2.6.0 to support SSL.

Exhibitor

  • hosts: initial list of Exhibitor (ZooKeeper) nodes in format: ‘host1,host2,etc…’. This list updates automatically whenever the Exhibitor (ZooKeeper) cluster topology changes.
  • poll_interval: how often the list of ZooKeeper and Exhibitor nodes should be updated from Exhibitor.
  • port: Exhibitor port.

Kubernetes

  • bypass_api_service: (optional) normally, Patroni contacts the kubernetes service at KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST. With bypass_api_service set to true, it discovers the API nodes behind the service and contacts them directly.
  • namespace: (optional) Kubernetes namespace where Patroni pod is running. Default value is default.
  • labels: labels in {label1: value1, label2: value2} format. Patroni uses them to find cluster Pods and Endpoints or ConfigMaps. It also adds them to each Endpoint or ConfigMap it creates.
  • scope_label: (optional) name of the label containing cluster name. Default value is cluster-name.
  • bootstrap_labels: (optional) labels in {label1: value1, label2: value2} format. Patroni assigns them to pods in these states: initializing new cluster, running custom bootstrap script, starting after custom bootstrap, or creating replica.
  • role_label: (optional) name of the label containing role (primary, replica, or other custom value). Patroni will set this label on the pod it runs in. Default value is role.
  • leader_label_value: (optional) value of the pod label when Postgres role is primary. Default value is primary.
  • follower_label_value: (optional) value of the pod label when Postgres role is replica. Default value is replica.
  • standby_leader_label_value: (optional) value of the pod label when Postgres role is standby_leader. Default value is primary.
  • tmp_role_label: (optional) name of the temporary label containing role (primary or replica). Value of this label will always use the default of corresponding role. Set only when necessary.
  • use_endpoints: (optional) if set to true, Patroni will use Endpoints instead of ConfigMaps to run leader elections and keep cluster state.
  • pod_ip: (optional) Patroni pod IP address. Required when use_endpoints is enabled. On promotion, Patroni uses this value to populate leader endpoint subsets.
  • ports: (optional) named Service ports require identical Endpoint port names. Otherwise, the service cannot work. For {Kind: Service, spec: {ports: [{name: postgresql, port: 5432, targetPort: 5432}]}}, set kubernetes.ports: [{"name": "postgresql", "port": 5432}]. Patroni uses this value to update leader Endpoint subsets. Applies only with kubernetes.use_endpoints.
  • cacert: (optional) CA_BUNDLE file with trusted CA certificates for Kubernetes API SSL verification. Defaults to the ServiceAccount secret value.
  • retriable_http_codes: (optional) Kubernetes API status codes to retry. By default, Patroni retries 500, 503, and 504, and responses with a retry-after HTTP header.

Raft (deprecated)

  • self_addr: ip:port to listen on for Raft connections. The self_addr must be accessible from other nodes of the cluster. If not set, the node will not participate in consensus.

  • bind_addr: (optional) ip:port to listen on for Raft connections. If not specified the self_addr will be used.

  • partner_addrs: list of other Patroni nodes in the cluster in format:

    ′ip1:port′,′ip2:port′,′etc...′'ip1:port', 'ip2:port', 'etc...'′ip1:port′,′ip2:port′,′etc...′

  • data_dir: directory where to store Raft log and snapshot. If not specified the current working directory is used.

  • password: (optional) Encrypt Raft traffic with a specified password, requires cryptography python module.

  • min_timeout: (optional) minimum election timeout in seconds for the underlying pysyncobj Raft implementation. Must be greater than 3 * append_entries_period. Default: 0.4.

  • max_timeout: (optional) maximum election timeout in seconds for the underlying pysyncobj Raft implementation. Must be greater than min_timeout. Default: 1.4.

  • connection_timeout: (optional) time in seconds after which a connection with no data received is considered dead. Must be greater than or equal to max_timeout. Default: 3.5.

  • append_entries_period: (optional) interval in seconds for sending heartbeat (append_entries) commands. Must be less than one-third of min_timeout. Default: 0.1.

  • connection_retry_time: (optional) interval in seconds between reconnection attempts to offline nodes. Default: 5.0.

  • leader_fallback_timeout: (optional) time in seconds after which a leader with no response from the majority falls back to follower state. Must be greater than append_entries_period. Default: 30.0.

Note

Adjust these timeouts for high-latency networks where pysyncobj defaults are too short. Required constraints: min_timeout > 3 * append_entries_period, max_timeout > min_timeout, connection_timeout >= max_timeout, and leader_fallback_timeout > append_entries_period. Patroni validates them at startup and refuses to start if they fail. Changes require a restart.

[!WARNING] These settings only extend pysyncobj election and connection timeouts. Each Raft command, including leader lock refresh and cluster-state writes, must still finish within retry_timeout (default 10). With round-trip latency above approximately a few seconds, a command can exceed this limit even if connection_timeout exceeds the RTT. The DCS then appears unreachable, and the primary may demote. Also increase retry_timeout and ttl on these links. Keep loop_wait + 2 * retry_timeout <= ttl.

Short FAQ about Raft implementation

  • Q: How to list all the nodes providing consensus?

    A: syncobj_admin -conn host:port -status where the host:port is the address of one of the cluster nodes

  • Q: Node that was a part of consensus and has gone and I can’t reuse the same IP for other node. How to remove this node from the consensus?

    A: syncobj_admin -conn host:port -remove host2:port2 where the host2:port2 is the address of the node you want to remove from consensus.

  • Q: Where to get the syncobj_admin utility?

    A: It is installed together with pysyncobj module (python RAFT implementation), which is Patroni dependency.

  • Q: it is possible to run Patroni node without adding in to the consensus?

    A: Yes, just comment out or remove raft.self_addr from Patroni configuration.

  • Q: It is possible to run Patroni and PostgreSQL only on two nodes?

    A: Yes, on the third node you can run patroni_raft_controller (without Patroni and PostgreSQL). In such a setup, one can temporarily lose one node without affecting the primary.

PostgreSQL

  • postgresql:

    • authentication:

      • superuser:
        • username: name for the superuser, set during initialization (initdb) and later used by Patroni to connect to the postgres.
        • password: password for the superuser, set during initialization (initdb).
        • sslmode: (optional) maps to sslmode. Specifies the TLS negotiation mode. See the PostgreSQL documentation for each mode. Default: prefer.
        • sslkey: (optional) maps to sslkey. Path to the secret key for the client certificate.
        • sslpassword: (optional) maps to sslpassword. Password for the secret key in sslkey.
        • sslcert: (optional) maps to sslcert. Path to the client certificate.
        • sslrootcert: (optional) maps to sslrootcert. File containing one or more trusted CA certificates. The client uses them to verify the server certificate.
        • sslcrl: (optional) maps to sslcrl. Certificate revocation list file. The client rejects servers whose certificates appear in the list.
        • sslcrldir: (optional) maps to sslcrldir. Directory containing certificate revocation list files. The client rejects servers whose certificates appear in these lists.
        • sslnegotiation: (optional) maps to sslnegotiation. Controls server SSL negotiation when SSL is used.
        • gssencmode: (optional) maps to gssencmode. Controls whether to negotiate secure GSS TCP/IP and its priority.
        • channel_binding: (optional) maps to channel_binding. Controls client channel binding.
      • replication:
        • username: replication username; the user will be created during initialization. Replicas will use this user to access the replication source via streaming replication
        • password: replication password; the user will be created during initialization.
        • sslmode: (optional) maps to sslmode. Specifies the TLS negotiation mode. See the PostgreSQL documentation for each mode. Default: prefer.
        • sslkey: (optional) maps to sslkey. Path to the secret key for the client certificate.
        • sslpassword: (optional) maps to sslpassword. Password for the secret key in sslkey.
        • sslcert: (optional) maps to sslcert. Path to the client certificate.
        • sslrootcert: (optional) maps to sslrootcert. File containing one or more trusted CA certificates. The client uses them to verify the server certificate.
        • sslcrl: (optional) maps to sslcrl. Certificate revocation list file. The client rejects servers whose certificates appear in the list.
        • sslcrldir: (optional) maps to sslcrldir. Directory containing certificate revocation list files. The client rejects servers whose certificates appear in these lists.
        • sslnegotiation: (optional) maps to sslnegotiation. Controls server SSL negotiation when SSL is used.
        • gssencmode: (optional) maps to gssencmode. Controls whether to negotiate secure GSS TCP/IP and its priority.
        • channel_binding: (optional) maps to channel_binding. Controls client channel binding.
      • rewind:
        • username: (optional) name for the user for pg_rewind; the user will be created during initialization of postgres 11+ and all necessary permissions will be granted.
        • password: (optional) password for the user for pg_rewind; the user will be created during initialization.
        • sslmode: (optional) maps to sslmode. Specifies the TLS negotiation mode. See the PostgreSQL documentation for each mode. Default: prefer.
        • sslkey: (optional) maps to sslkey. Path to the secret key for the client certificate.
        • sslpassword: (optional) maps to sslpassword. Password for the secret key in sslkey.
        • sslcert: (optional) maps to sslcert. Path to the client certificate.
        • sslrootcert: (optional) maps to sslrootcert. File containing one or more trusted CA certificates. The client uses them to verify the server certificate.
        • sslcrl: (optional) maps to sslcrl. Certificate revocation list file. The client rejects servers whose certificates appear in the list.
        • sslcrldir: (optional) maps to sslcrldir. Directory containing certificate revocation list files. The client rejects servers whose certificates appear in these lists.
        • sslnegotiation: (optional) maps to sslnegotiation. Controls server SSL negotiation when SSL is used.
        • gssencmode: (optional) maps to gssencmode. Controls whether to negotiate secure GSS TCP/IP and its priority.
        • channel_binding: (optional) maps to channel_binding. Controls client channel binding.

    • callbacks: callback scripts to run on certain actions. Patroni will pass the action, role and cluster name. (See scripts/aws.py as an example of how to write them.)

      • on_reload: run this script when configuration reload is triggered.
      • on_restart: run this script when the postgres restarts (without changing role).
      • on_role_change: run this script when the postgres is being promoted or demoted.
      • on_start: run this script when the postgres starts.
      • on_stop: run this script when the postgres stops.

    • connect_address: IP address + port through which Postgres is accessible from other nodes and applications.

    • proxy_address: address and port of the connection pool beside Postgres, such as pgbouncer. Patroni stores it as proxy_url in the DCS member key for service discovery.

    • create_replica_methods: ordered methods for creating replicas. The default is “basebackup”. Other methods refer to scripts, each with its own configuration section. See custom replica creation methods.

    • data_dir: The location of the Postgres data directory, either existing or to be initialized by Patroni.

    • config_dir: The location of the Postgres configuration directory, defaults to the data directory. Must be writable by Patroni.

    • bin_dir: (optional) Path to PostgreSQL binaries (pg_ctl, initdb, pg_controldata, pg_basebackup, postgres, pg_isready, pg_rewind). If not provided or is an empty string, PATH environment variable will be used to find the executables.

    • bin_name: (optional) Make it possible to override Postgres binary names, if you are using a custom Postgres distribution:

      • pg_ctl: (optional) Custom name for pg_ctl binary.
      • initdb: (optional) Custom name for initdb binary.
      • pgcontroldata: (optional) Custom name for pg_controldata binary.
      • pg_basebackup: (optional) Custom name for pg_basebackup binary.
      • postgres: (optional) Custom name for postgres binary.
      • pg_isready: (optional) Custom name for pg_isready binary.
      • pg_rewind: (optional) Custom name for pg_rewind binary.

    • listen: Postgres listen addresses and port. With streaming replication, other cluster nodes must be able to reach this address. Separate multiple addresses with commas and append the port after the last address: listen: 127.0.0.1,127.0.0.2:5432. Patroni uses the first address for local connections.

    • use_unix_socket: prefer Unix sockets for cluster connections. Default: false. If unix_socket_directories is set, use its first suitable value, or TCP if none is suitable. If absent from postgresql.parameters, assume the default socket directory and omit host from connection parameters.

    • use_unix_socket_repl: prefer Unix sockets for replication user connections. Default: false. If unix_socket_directories is set, use its first suitable value, or TCP if none is suitable. If absent from postgresql.parameters, assume the default socket directory and omit host from connection parameters.

    • pgpass: path to the .pgpass password file. Patroni creates this file before executing pg_basebackup, the post_init script and under some other circumstances. The location must be writable by Patroni.

    • recovery_conf: additional configuration settings written to recovery.conf when configuring follower.

    • custom_conf : optional custom postgresql.conf file to use instead of postgresql.base.conf. It must exist on all nodes and be readable by PostgreSQL. The actual postgresql.conf includes it from its location. Patroni neither monitors nor backs up this file. Patroni configuration can still override its settings; see dynamic configuration.

    • parameters: configuration parameters (GUCs) for Postgres in format {ssl: "on", ssl_cert_file: "cert_file"}.

    • parameters_primary: (optional) role-specific parameter overrides for primary. These values are merged with and override the base parameters.

    • parameters_replica: (optional) role-specific parameter overrides for replica. These values are merged with and override the base parameters.

    • parameters_standby_leader: (optional) role-specific parameter overrides for standby_leader. These values are merged with and override the base parameters.

    • pg_hba: list of lines that Patroni will use to generate pg_hba.conf. Patroni ignores this parameter if hba_file PostgreSQL parameter is set to a non-default value. Together with dynamic configuration this parameter simplifies management of pg_hba.conf.

      • - host all all 0.0.0.0/0 md5
      • - host replication replicator 127.0.0.1/32 md5: A line like this is required for replication.

    • pg_hba_primary: (optional) role-specific pg_hba entries for primary. These completely replace pg_hba (no merging). If not defined, pg_hba is used.

    • pg_hba_replica: (optional) role-specific pg_hba entries for replica. These completely replace pg_hba (no merging). If not defined, pg_hba is used.

    • pg_hba_standby_leader: (optional) role-specific pg_hba entries for standby_leader. These completely replace pg_hba (no merging). If not defined, pg_hba is used.

    • pg_ident: list of lines that Patroni will use to generate pg_ident.conf. Patroni ignores this parameter if ident_file PostgreSQL parameter is set to a non-default value. Together with dynamic configuration this parameter simplifies management of pg_ident.conf.

      • - mapname1 systemname1 pguser1
      • - mapname1 systemname2 pguser2

    • pg_ident_primary: (optional) role-specific pg_ident entries for primary. These completely replace pg_ident (no merging). If not defined, pg_ident is used.

    • pg_ident_replica: (optional) role-specific pg_ident entries for replica. These completely replace pg_ident (no merging). If not defined, pg_ident is used.

    • pg_ident_standby_leader: (optional) role-specific pg_ident entries for standby_leader. These completely replace pg_ident (no merging). If not defined, pg_ident is used.

    • pg_ctl_timeout: How long should pg_ctl wait when doing start, stop or restart. Default value is 60 seconds.

    • use_pg_rewind: try pg_rewind when the old leader rejoins as a replica. For correct operation, pg_rewind requires data page checksums and/or wal_log_hints set to on. Enable checksums with the --data-checksums option for initdb.

    • rewind: (optional) custom options to pass to the pg_rewind command. Can be specified as a list of strings and/or single key-value dictionaries. Not allowed options include: target-pgdata, source-pgdata, source-server, write-recovery-conf, dry-run, restore-target-wal, config-file, no-ensure-shutdown, version, and help. Example usage:

      postgresql:
  rewind:
    - debug
    - progress
    - sync-method: fsync

    • remove_data_directory_on_rewind_failure: If this option is enabled, Patroni will remove the PostgreSQL data directory and recreate the replica. Otherwise it will try to follow the new leader. Default value is false.

    • remove_data_directory_on_diverged_timelines: remove the data directory and recreate the replica if timelines diverge and the old primary cannot stream from the new primary. Use this when pg_rewind is unavailable. On PostgreSQL v10 and older, Patroni checks divergence by connecting to the “postgres” database with replication credentials. Permit this access in pg_hba.conf. Default: false.

    • replica_method: add a section for each create_replica_methods entry other than basebackup. Use the method name as the section name. At minimum, set “command” to the full script path. Patroni passes other parameters to the script as “parameter=value”.

    • pre_promote: fencing script run after leader lock acquisition and before replica promotion during failover. A non-zero exit prevents promotion and removes the DCS leader key.

    • before_stop: script run immediately before Postgres stops. It runs synchronously and blocks shutdown until completion. Shutdown then proceeds regardless of its exit code.

REST API

  • restapi:
    • thread_pool_size: size of thread pool used by Patroni to process REST API requests. Minimal value is 5, default value is 5.
    • connect_address: address or host name and port for the REST API. Every cluster member must reach it. Use a non-loopback address except for a local demonstration; “localhost” and “127.0.0.1” are unsuitable for other deployments. It supports HTTP health checks, user queries, and member checks during leader elections. These checks determine whether the leader runs or another node has a later WAL position. Patroni stores connect_address in the DCS member key so member names resolve to REST API addresses.
    • listen: address or host name and port for REST API health checks and communication between cluster members. HAProxy and other load balancers can use HTTP “OPTION” or “GET” checks.
    • authentication: (optional)
      • username: Basic-auth username to protect unsafe REST API endpoints.
      • password: Basic-auth password to protect unsafe REST API endpoints.
    • certfile: (optional): Specifies the file with the certificate in the PEM format. If the certfile is not specified or is left empty, the API server will work without SSL.
    • keyfile: (optional): Specifies the file with the secret key in the PEM format.
    • keyfile_password: (optional): Specifies a password for decrypting the keyfile.
    • cafile: (optional): Specifies the file with the CA_BUNDLE with certificates of trusted CAs to use while verifying client certs.
    • ciphers: (optional): Specifies the permitted cipher suites (e.g. “ECDHE-RSA-AES256-GCM-SHA384:DHE-RSA-AES256-GCM-SHA384:ECDHE-RSA-AES128-GCM-SHA256:DHE-RSA-AES128-GCM-SHA256:!SSLv1:!SSLv2:!SSLv3:!TLSv1:!TLSv1.1”)
    • verify_client: (optional) client certificate verification mode: none (default), optional, or required. none disables verification. required requires certificates for all calls; successful signature verification authenticates the client. optional requires and checks certificates only for unsafe PUT, POST, PATCH, and DELETE requests.
    • allowlist: (optional) hosts permitted to call unsafe endpoints. Each entry can be a host name, IP address, or CIDR network. Default: allow all. With allowlist or allowlist_include_members set, all unlisted sources are rejected.
    • allowlist_include_members: (optional) if true, permits unsafe endpoint access from cluster members registered in the DCS. It uses each member’s api_url host name or IP address. The operating system may use a different address for outgoing connections.
    • http_extra_headers: (optional): HTTP headers let the REST API server pass additional information with an HTTP response.
    • https_extra_headers: (optional): HTTPS headers let the REST API server pass additional information with an HTTP response when TLS is enabled. This will also pass additional information set in http_extra_headers.
    • request_queue_size: (optional): Sets request queue size for TCP socket used by Patroni REST API. Once the queue is full, further requests get a “Connection denied” error. The default value is 5.
    • server_tokens: (optional): Configures the value of the Server HTTP header.
      • Minimal: The header will contain only the Patroni version, e.g. Patroni/4.0.0.
      • ProductOnly: The header will contain only the product name, e.g. Patroni.
      • Original (default): The header will expose the original behaviour and display the BaseHTTP and Python versions, e.g. BaseHTTP/0.6 Python/3.12.3.

Here is an example of both http_extra_headers and https_extra_headers:

restapi:
  listen: <listen>
  connect_address: <connect_address>
  authentication:
    username: <username>
    password: <password>
  http_extra_headers:
    'X-Frame-Options': 'SAMEORIGIN'
    'X-XSS-Protection': '1; mode=block'
    'X-Content-Type-Options': 'nosniff'
  cafile: <ca file>
  certfile: <cert>
  keyfile: <key>
  https_extra_headers:
    'Strict-Transport-Security': 'max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains'

Warning

  • The restapi.connect_address must be accessible from all nodes of a given Patroni cluster. Internally Patroni is using it during the leader race to find nodes with minimal replication lag.
  • If you enabled client certificates validation (restapi.verify_client is set to required), you also must provide valid client certificates in the ctl.certfile, ctl.keyfile, ctl.keyfile_password. If not provided, Patroni will not work correctly.

CTL

  • ctl: (optional)
    • authentication:
      • username: Basic-auth username for accessing protected REST API endpoints. If not provided patronictl will use the value provided for REST API “username” parameter.
      • password: Basic-auth password for accessing protected REST API endpoints. If not provided patronictl will use the value provided for REST API “password” parameter.
    • insecure: Allow connections to REST API without verifying SSL certs.
    • cacert: CA_BUNDLE file or directory with trusted CAs for REST API SSL verification. If absent, patronictl uses the REST API “cafile” value.
    • certfile: Specifies the file with the client certificate in the PEM format.
    • keyfile: Specifies the file with the client secret key in the PEM format.
    • keyfile_password: Specifies a password for decrypting the client keyfile.

Watchdog

  • mode: off, automatic, or required. off disables the watchdog. automatic uses it when available and otherwise ignores it. required prevents leadership unless watchdog activation succeeds.
  • device: Path to watchdog device. Defaults to /dev/watchdog.
  • safety_margin: Number of seconds of safety margin between watchdog triggering and leader key expiration.

Tags

  • clonefrom: true or false. If true, other nodes may prefer this node as their bootstrap pg_basebackup source. With multiple nodes tagged clonefrom, selection is random. Default: false.
  • noloadbalance: true or false. If set to true the node will return HTTP Status Code 503 for the GET /replica REST API health-check and therefore will be excluded from the load-balancing. Defaults to false.
  • replicatefrom: The name of another replica to replicate from. Used to support cascading replication.
  • nosync: true or false. If set to true the node will never be selected as a synchronous replica.
  • sync_priority: integer priority for synchronous standby selection with synchronous_mode set to on. Higher values are preferred. Values of 0 or less exclude the node from synchronous_standby_names, like nosync: true. This is the opposite of sync_priority in pg_stat_replication.
  • nofailover: true or false, controls whether this node is allowed to participate in the leader race and become a leader. Defaults to false, meaning this node can_ participate in leader races.
  • failover_priority: integer failover priority. Higher values are preferred when nodes have received/replayed the same WAL amount. A higher receive/replay LSN takes precedence over priority. Values of 0 or less prevent leader election participation and promotion, like nofailover: true. Currently, failover_priority does not work with quorum-based synchronous replication.
  • nostream: true or false. If true, disable WAL streaming through the replication protocol. Use archive recovery, if restore_command is configured, and pg_wal/pg_xlog polling instead. Also disable permanent logical slot copying and synchronization on this node and its cascading replicas. This tag has no effect on a primary.
Warning

Provide only one of nofailover or failover_priority. Providing nofailover: true is the same as failover_priority: 0, and providing nofailover: false will give the node priority 1.

In addition to these predefined tags, you can also add your own ones:

  • key1: true
  • key2: false
  • key3: 1.4
  • key4: "RandomString"

Tags appear in the REST API and patronictl_list. Use them in instance health checks. An undefined tag or a value different from the query value returns HTTP 503.

3.3 - Environment Configuration Settings

Environment variables for overriding Patroni configuration parameters.

Source: https://patroni.readthedocs.io/en/latest/ENVIRONMENT.html

System environment variables can override some Patroni configuration file parameters. This page lists all supported variables. Environment values always take precedence over file values.

Global/Universal

  • PATRONI_CONFIGURATION: sets the complete Patroni configuration through PATRONI_CONFIGURATION. Patroni then ignores all other environment variables.
  • PATRONI_THREAD_POOL_SIZE: thread pool size for asynchronous tasks and REST API communication with other members during leader races or failsafe checks. Minimum: 5. Default: 5.
  • PATRONI_THREAD_STACK_SIZE: stack size for Patroni threads. Use a multiple of 64kB. Minimum: 64kB. Patroni default: 512kB.
  • PATRONI_NAME: name of the node where the current instance of Patroni is running. Must be unique for the cluster. The value __patroni_strict_sync_replica_placeholder__ is reserved for internal use by Patroni and cannot be used as a node name.
  • PATRONI_NAMESPACE: path within the configuration store where Patroni will keep information about the cluster. Default value: “/service”
  • PATRONI_SCOPE: cluster name
  • PG_MALLOC_ARENA_MAX: custom value for MALLOC_ARENA_MAX environment variable for postmaster process. If not set, postmaster will inherit MALLOC_ARENA_MAX value.

Log

  • PATRONI_LOG_TYPE: sets the format of logs. Can be either plain or json. To use json format, you must have the jsonlogger installed. The default value is plain.
  • PATRONI_LOG_LEVEL: sets the general logging level. Default value is INFO (see the docs for Python logging)
  • PATRONI_LOG_TRACEBACK_LEVEL: sets the level where tracebacks will be visible. Default value is ERROR. Set it to DEBUG if you want to see tracebacks only if you enable PATRONI_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG.
  • PATRONI_LOG_FORMAT: log format. For plain logs, use a string. For json, use a string or a list of LogRecord attributes. In lists, use field names without %( and ). To rename a field, use a dictionary with the original field as key and the output name as value. Default: %(asctime)s %(levelname)s: %(message)s.
  • PATRONI_LOG_DATEFORMAT: sets the datetime formatting string. (see the formatTime() documentation)
  • PATRONI_LOG_STATIC_FIELDS: add additional fields to the log. This option is only available when the log type is set to json. Example PATRONI_LOG_STATIC_FIELDS="{app: patroni}"
  • PATRONI_LOG_MAX_QUEUE_SIZE: maximum internal log queue size. Patroni writes records to an in-memory queue. A separate thread reads them and writes to stderr or a file. The default 1000 records retain approximately 1h20m of logs.
  • PATRONI_LOG_DIR: application log directory. It must exist and be writable by the Patroni user. By default, Patroni retains four 25MB files. Adjust retention with PATRONI_LOG_FILE_NUM and PATRONI_LOG_FILE_SIZE.
  • PATRONI_LOG_MODE: Permissions for log files (for example, 0644). If not specified, permissions will be set based on the current umask value.
  • PATRONI_LOG_FILE_NUM: The number of application logs to retain.
  • PATRONI_LOG_FILE_SIZE: Size of patroni.log file (in bytes) that triggers a log rolling.
  • PATRONI_LOG_LOGGERS: Redefine logging level per python module. Example PATRONI_LOG_LOGGERS="{patroni.postmaster: WARNING, urllib3: DEBUG}"
  • PATRONI_LOG_DEDUPLICATE_HEARTBEAT_LOGS: suppresses consecutive identical heartbeat messages when true. Default: false.
Warning

HA loop timing helps diagnose failovers caused by exhausted resources and similar problems. With PATRONI_LOG_DEDUPLICATE_HEARTBEAT_LOGS set to true, loop execution produces no log unless the leader changes. These diagnostics are then unavailable in the logs.

Citus

Enable Citus integration. Patroni then registers workers on the coordinator. See Citus support.

  • PATRONI_CITUS_GROUP: the Citus group id, integer. Use 0 for coordinator and 1, 2, etc… for workers
  • PATRONI_CITUS_DATABASE: database for the citus extension. Use the same database on the coordinator and all workers. Currently, only one database is supported.

Consul

  • PATRONI_CONSUL_HOST: the host:port for the Consul local agent.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_URL: url for the Consul local agent, in format: http(s)://host:port
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_PORT: (optional) Consul port
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_SCHEME: (optional) http or https, defaults to http
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_TOKEN: (optional) ACL token
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_VERIFY: (optional) whether to verify the SSL certificate for HTTPS requests
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_CACERT: (optional) The ca certificate. If present it will enable validation.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_CERT: (optional) File with the client certificate
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_KEY: (optional) File with the client key. Can be empty if the key is part of certificate.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_DC: (optional) Datacenter to communicate with. By default the datacenter of the host is used.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_CONSISTENCY: (optional) Select consul consistency mode. Possible values are default, consistent, or stale (more details in consul API reference)
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_CHECKS: (optional) list of Consul health checks used for the session. By default an empty list is used.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_REGISTER_SERVICE: (optional) registers a service named by scope. Its role tag is master, primary, replica, or standby-leader. Default: false.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_SERVICE_TAGS: (optional) additional static tags to add to the Consul service apart from the role (primary/replica/standby-leader). By default an empty list is used.
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_SERVICE_CHECK_INTERVAL: (optional) how often to perform health check against registered url
  • PATRONI_CONSUL_SERVICE_CHECK_TLS_SERVER_NAME: (optional) override SNI host when connecting via TLS, see also consul agent check API reference.

Etcd

  • PATRONI_ETCD_PROXY: proxy url for the etcd. If you are connecting to the etcd using proxy, use this parameter instead of PATRONI_ETCD_URL
  • PATRONI_ETCD_URL: url for the etcd, in format: http(s)://(username:password@)host:port
  • PATRONI_ETCD_HOSTS: list of etcd endpoints in format ‘host1:port1’,‘host2:port2’,etc…
  • PATRONI_ETCD_USE_PROXIES: if true, treats hosts as a fixed proxy list. Patroni does not discover etcd topology.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_PROTOCOL: http or https, if not specified http is used. If the url or proxy is specified - will take protocol from them.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_HOST: the host:port for the etcd endpoint.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_SRV: domain for SRV-based cluster discovery. Patroni queries these services in order until one succeeds: _etcd-client-ssl, _etcd-client, _etcd-ssl, _etcd, _etcd-server-ssl, _etcd-server. For _etcd-server-ssl or _etcd-server, it queries available members through the ETCD peer protocol. Otherwise, it uses hosts from the SRV records.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_SRV_SUFFIX: suffix for discovery SRV names. Requires PATRONI_ETCD_SRV. Use it to distinguish etcd clusters in one domain. For example, PATRONI_ETCD_SRV_SUFFIX=foo and PATRONI_ETCD_SRV=example.org produce _etcd-client-ssl-foo._tcp.example.com. Patroni applies this suffix to each possible ETCD SRV service name.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_USERNAME: username for etcd authentication.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_PASSWORD: password for etcd authentication.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_CACERT: The ca certificate. If present it will enable validation.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_CERT: File with the client certificate.
  • PATRONI_ETCD_KEY: File with the client key. Can be empty if the key is part of certificate.

Etcdv3

For Etcdv3, replace ETCD with ETCD3 in Etcd variable names. Examples include PATRONI_ETCD3_HOST and PATRONI_ETCD3_CACERT.

Warning

Protocol versions 2 and 3 cannot see each other’s keys. Changing Patroni configuration alone cannot migrate Etcd to Etcdv3. Patroni uses the Etcd gRPC-gateway proxy for the V3 API. This prevents TLS common name authentication.

ZooKeeper

  • PATRONI_ZOOKEEPER_HOSTS: Comma separated list of ZooKeeper cluster members: “‘host1:port1’,‘host2:port2’,’etc…’”. It is important to quote every single entity!
  • PATRONI_ZOOKEEPER_USE_SSL: (optional) Whether SSL is used or not. Defaults to false. If set to false, all SSL specific parameters are ignored.
  • PATRONI_ZOOKEEPER_CACERT: (optional) The CA certificate. If present it will enable validation.
  • PATRONI_ZOOKEEPER_CERT: (optional) File with the client certificate.
